[www.nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/)\n\nAs well as\nonline train times and fares, [www.nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/)\nhas:\n\n- details of any major\ndisruption and a 'live departure board' system showing\nwhether trains are running on time.\n\n- telephone\nnumbers and addresses for lost property, travel\nassistance, current train\nrunning information, telesales and customer\nservices departments for every train operator in Britain.\n\n- general information for travelling on the UK national\nrail network.\n\n- details of station facilities.\n\n- links\nto each train operator's own website.\n\n- There are left luggage\noffices at all the major London stations, also at\nBirmingham New Street, Edinburgh Waverley, Glasgow Central,\nLeeds, Liverpool Lime St, and Manchester Piccadilly.\nSee\n[www.left-baggage.co.uk](https://www.left-baggage.co.uk/).  There's also a small\noffice at Holyhead.  Most other stations don't have\nleft luggage facilities.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Are the trains running on time?  Are there engineering works?\n\n- Go to\n[www.nationalrail.co.uk/live-trains](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/live-trains/), enter the name of your station to see a\n'live departure board' showing train arrivals & departures in the next hour or\ntwo, and whether they are on time or not.\n\n- Advanced users may find the website\n[www.realtimetrains.co.uk](https://www.realtimetrains.co.uk/) useful.  If you look up a train and click for\nthe detailed version, it shows rail mileages.  It holds actual on-time\nperformance data for the past 7 days.  You can check the progress of a\ntrain that's currently running, and it even shows the booked rolling stock type\nand train formation.\n\n- _Tip:_  At major\nstations including the London termini, platform numbers are only posted when\nthey are confirmed, which might only be 10-30 minutes before departure.  However,\n[www.realtimetrains.co.uk](https://www.realtimetrains.co.uk/) and phone app\n[UK Live Trains](https://apps.apple.com/gb/app/uk-live-train-times/id1063706811) show the _planned_ platform before the _actual_\nplatform is posted, unlike most official apps and the station departure boards\nwhich keep you guessing until the last moment.  If you know in advance what\nplatform your train is _expected_ to use, it reduces stress and you can\nposition yourself ready near that platform.  Just remember that the actual\nplatform sometimes differs from the planned one!\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Train company websites\n\n- Go to\n[www.nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/), scroll right to the bottom and\nclick 'Train Operating Companies' for each train company's website address, postal address &\nphone numbers.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### First  or Standard class?\n\nMost leisure travellers go\nstandard class, with first class used by businessmen.  But if you pre-book\nyou can find some great first class deals.  First class fares on inter-city\ntrains run by Avanti West Coast, LNER and East Midlands Trains include light meals\n&\ndrinks, at least on Mondays-Fridays.  GWR & Cross-Country first class\nincludes complimentary tea & coffee on their inter-city routes.  However,\nyour default assumption on other routes should be that first class simple means\nnicer seats and more room.\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| [![1st class seats on an Avanti West Coast pendolino](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-first-class.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-first-class-large.jpg) |  | [![Standard class seats on an Avanti West Coast pendolino](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-standard-class.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-standard-class-large.jpg) |\n\nThis is **1st**\n**class** (above left) and **standard class** (above right) on a Pendolino\nfrom London to Birmingham, Manchester, Liverpool, the Lake District, Carlisle\nand Glasgow, run by Avanti West Coast.  As you can see, in standard class\nthere are 2+2 seats across the car width, in first class it's 1+2, so there's\nmore elbow room, more legroom, and there are tables for two and solo seats\n(below left).  Avanti West Coast is unique in offering a middle class\nbetween standard and first called Standard Premium, which uses exactly the same\nseating as 1st class but without any food or drink included.\n\n**Food included in 1st class on LNER, Avanti West Coast & East Midlands Trains:**\nBelow right, a light meal and wine served on an LNER Azuma train from London to\nEdinburgh, included in the fare.  Avanti West Coast & East Midlands Trains\noffer a similar 1st class food & drink service.  Another good\nreason for taking train not plane between London and Edinburgh, Glasgow, Manchester\nor Newcastle!\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| [![First class table for two on an LNER Azuma](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-table-for-two.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-table-for-two-large.jpg) |  | [![Complimentary light meal on an LNER train from London to Edinburgh](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-food.jpg)](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-train-food-large.jpg) |\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### London  Underground  & Bus information\n\n- [tfl.gov.uk](https://tfl.gov.uk/)\nfor London Underground, bus, Docklands Light Railway,\nCroydon Tramlink and other London transport\ninformation.\n\n- [tfl.gov.uk/modes/tube](https://tfl.gov.uk/modes/tube/)\nfor London Underground information.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Need a bus at the other end?\n\n- [www.traveline.info](https://www.traveline.info/)\nallows you to check local bus times across the UK,\nor you can call Traveline on 0871 200 22 33.  Traveline\nis a national\ninitiative to make local travel information available on a\nsingle number nationwide.  All call centres open\n08:00-20:00, some open longer.\n\n- There is a\nsystem called PLUSBUS, where for £2-£3 more than the normal\ntrain fare you can add unlimited bus travel within your\norigin and/or destination city.  So for example,\ninstead of buying a normal return from London to Manchester\nfor (say) £98, you can buy a return to _Manchester Plusbus_\nfor just £3 or so more and get exactly the same train ticket\nbut with unlimited free bus travel within central Manchester\nfor the day added on.  See\n[www.plusbus.info](https://www.plusbus.info/) for\ndetails of the 200 towns and cities in which PLUSBUS\noperates, or just ask when you buy your train ticket.\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Need a ferry  at the other end?\n\n- Ferries to the Isle of\nWight: [www.wightlink.co.uk](https://www.wightlink.co.uk/)\nfor Portsmouth-Ryde and Lymington-Yarmouth,\n[www.redfunnel.co.uk](https://www.redfunnel.co.uk/)\nfor Southampton-Cowes,\n[www.hovertravel.co.uk](https://www.hovertravel.co.uk/) for hovercraft Portsmouth-Ryde.\nYou can buy through train & ferry tickets between any\nmainland station and any isle of Wight station using any train operator site\nsuch as\n[tfw.wales](https://www.seat61.com/booking/tfw.htm).\n\n- Ferries\nto Jersey, Guernsey & the Channel Islands, [see the Channel Islands page](https://www.seat61.com/train-and-ferry-to-jersey-and-guernsey.htm).\n\n- Ferries to the\nIsle of Man, [see the Isle of \\\\\nMan page](https://www.seat61.com/train-and-ferry-to-the-isle-of-man.htm).\n\n- Ferry to the\nScilly Isles:\n[www.islesofscilly-travel.co.uk](https://www.islesofscilly-travel.co.uk/), also [see the seat61 Cornwall page](https://www.seat61.com/sleeper-to-cornwall.htm).\n\n- Ferries to Skye and the\nHebrides - Caledonian MacBrayne,\n[www.calmac.co.uk](https://www.calmac.co.uk/).\n\n- Ferries to Orkney and the\nShetland Islands (Scrabster-Stromness and Aberdeen-Lerwick),\n[www.northlinkferries.co.uk](https://www.northlinkferries.co.uk/).\n\n- Train+ferry\ntravel to Belfast & Northern Ireland, [see the Northern Ireland page](https://www.seat61.com/train-and-ferry-to-belfast.htm).\n\n- Train+ferry\ntravel to Dublin & the Republic of Ireland, [see the Ireland page](https://www.seat61.com/train-and-ferry-to-dublin.htm).\n\n- Train+ferry\ntravel to Amsterdam & the Netherlands,\n[see the \\\\\nStena Line Rail & Sail page](https://www.seat61.com/trains-and-routes/london-to-amsterdam-by-ferry.htm).\n\n\nFor ferries to\n[France](https://www.seat61.com/France.htm),\n[Spain](https://www.seat61.com/Spain.htm),\n[Belgium](https://www.seat61.com/Belgium.htm), [Denmark](https://www.seat61.com/Denmark.htm),\n[Norway](https://www.seat61.com/Norway.htm)\n& [Sweden](https://www.seat61.com/Sweden.htm), see the relevant country page.\n\n### To Orkney & Shetland\n\nNorthlink Ferries ( [www.northlinkferries.co.uk](https://www.northlinkferries.co.uk/))\nsail from Scrabster near Thurso to Stromness in Orkney ( [see \\\\\nthe Orkney page](https://www.seat61.com/places-of-interest/train-and-ferry-to-orkney.htm)), and\nfrom Aberdeen to Kirkwall on Orkney & Lerwick on Shetland.\nAberdeen-Shetland has a daily overnight service, the ships are little gems, with\ncosy cabins, lounges, bar, restaurant, and an excellent premium _Magnus Lounge_\nwhich is well worth the extra charge.  All cabins have toilet & shower, premium cabins include access to the Magnus Lounge.  Northlink's terminal in Aberdeen is in central Aberdeen, an easy 5-10 minute\nwalk from Aberdeen station.\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Lounge on ferry](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-northlink-ferry-lounge.jpg) |  | ![Northlink ferry](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-northlink-ferry-bows.jpg) |\n| The premium _Magnus_<br>_Lounge_ on the _Hrossey_. |  | The _Hrossey_ ready to sail <br>from Aberdeen. |\n\n|     |     |     |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| ![Northlink ferry](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-northlink-ferry-logo.jpg) |  | ![Cabin on Northlink ferry](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-northlink-ferry-cabin.jpg) |\n| Northlink's distinctive Viking <br>logo. |  | A premium cabin on the _Hrossey_. |\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Taking your bike?\n\nMost trains carry bicycles, sometimes for free, sometimes for a\nsmall fee with advance reservation required.\n\n- Details\ncan also be found at\n[www.nationalrail.co.uk/on-the-train/train-travel-with-bicycles](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/on-the-train/train-travel-with-bicycles/)  and on individual train company\nwebsites.\n\n- For information on taking your bike on Eurostar & other\nEuropean trains, [see the Taking your bike to Europe by \\\\\ntrain page](https://www.seat61.com/bike-by-train.htm).\n\n- Only a few operators will carry tandems, for advice on train travel with tandems\nsee\n[tandem-club.org.uk](https://tandem-club.org.uk/files/information/trains/index.html).\n\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### Rail Rovers:  _Unlimited train travel for_ _anyone_\n\nRail Rovers give unlimited\ntravel over all or selected parts of the British rail network and can be bought\nby anyone, including UK residents, see\n[www.nationalrail.co.uk/.../ranger-tickets-and-rover-tickets](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/tickets-railcards-and-offers/ticket-types/ranger-tickets-and-rover-tickets/)\nor\n[www.gwr.com/your-tickets/choosing-your-ticket/rangers-and-rovers](https://www.gwr.com/your-tickets/choosing-your-ticket/rangers-and-rovers).  There are rail rovers\ncovering the whole network or just certain areas.  The All-Line Rail Rover\ngives unlimited travel over the whole British national rail network.\n\n**2026 All Line Rail Rover**\n**prices:**\n\n- 7-day\nstandard class All-Line Rail Rover £625 (child under 16 half price,\n33.3% off with 16-25/senior/two together/forces/disabled railcard)\n\n- 14-day\nstandard class All-Line Rail Rover £949 (child under 16 half price, 33.3% off with 16-25/senior/two\ntogether/forces/disabled\nrailcard)\n\n- 7-day 1st\nclass All-Line Rail Rover £949 (child under 16 half price, 33.3% off for senior/two\ntogether/forces/disabled railcard holders)\n\n- 14-day 1st\nclass All-Line Rail Rover £1,450 (child under 16 half price, 33.3% off for\nsenior/disabled/two\ntogether railcard\nholders)\n\n\n**How to buy a rail rover**\n\nYou can buy an All-Line Rail\nRover at [ticket.tpexpress.co.uk/search](https://ticket.tpexpress.co.uk/search) (click _other_).  This sells 14 day\nstandard class ones, but for some reason not 7 day.\n\nAlternatively, you can buy Rail Rovers from any railway station or train operator telesales\nline.  You can only buy one from stations within 3 days of the date you\nwant them to start.  If you have any trouble buying a rail rover, ask them\nto look in section G of their fares manual.\n\n**Travel restrictions**\n\nThe All-Line\nrover used to be valid on all national rail trains without\ntime restrictions but some restrictions were introduced\n**in**\n**2011** to stop business travellers using it on\ncertain key business routes.  No restrictions apply on Saturdays, Sundays and\nBank Holidays, but on Mondays to Fridays the\nAll-Line Rail Rover\nis not valid for boarding or alighting\ntrain services operated by Avanti West Coast, LNER, East Midlands Trains or Arriva CrossCountry at the following\nstations before 10:00:\n\nLondon Euston\n\nLondon Kings Cross\n\nLondon St Pancras International\n\nBirmingham New Street\n\nBedford\n\nLuton\n\nLuton Airport Parkway\n\nMilton Keynes Central\n\nStevenage\n\nWatford Junction\n\nThere are no restrictions on these companies'\ntrains at other stations or on Saturdays,\nSundays and holidays, or on other companies'\ntrains at any station on any day.  So it's no problem\nusing a Great Western train getting in to\nLondon before 10:00, or a ScotRail sleeper\narriving at London Euston before 10:00.\n\nHow to get around these new restrictions from\nEdinburgh, Newcastle, York, Leeds etc:\nYou cannot use your Rail Rover on a weekday from\n(say) Edinburgh or Newcastle to London if it\narrives in London before 10:00, but you can\nuse it on the same train as far as Peterborough\nwhere no alighting restrictions apply, then use\na suburban train run by First Capital Connect\nfor the rest of the journey to London, even if\nthis arrives in London before 10:00.\n\n\nAll-Line Rail Rovers are also valid on the Ffestiniog Railway, and on Caledonian\nsleepers (seated cars free, sleepers if you pay the berth\nsupplement).  They are not valid on Eurostar, Heathrow\nExpress, Heathrow Connect, London Underground or Docklands,\nheritage railways (except Ffestiniog Railways) or shipping\nservices.  For information on rail rovers covering\nother areas call National Rail Enquiries on 03457 48 49 50.\n\nIf you buy a rover using a\n2-Together railcard, you also can't use the pass before 09:30 Monday-Fridays.\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n### [![BritRail train passes for overseas visitors](https://www.seat61.com/images/UK-Britrail.gif)](https://klook.prf.hn/click/camref:1011l3BvPi/destination:https:/www.klook.com/en-GB/activity/10297-britrail-consecutive-rail-pass/)BritRail passes: _Unlimited train travel for overseas visitors_\n\nIf you\nlive overseas and plan to visit the UK, you can buy a\n'BritRail' pass which gives unlimited train travel on all 20+ British train\noperators for various periods.  It's now available as a mobile pass that\nyou can buy & download to your phone.\n\n- [BritRail prices, information \\\\\n& buy online](https://klook.prf.hn/click/camref:1011l3BvPi/destination:https:/www.klook.com/en-GB/activity/10297-britrail-consecutive-rail-pass/)\n\n- _Tip:_  Compare BritRail prices with\n[Interrail pass prices](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm#global-pass-types-and-prices), as a global\n[Interrail pass](https://www.seat61.com/how-to-use-an-interrail-pass.htm) also gives unlimited unrestricted\ntravel on all National Rail routes and operators in Britain.\n\n\n**BritRail pass or**\n**point-to-point tickets?**\n\nA pass is only worth it if you're\ngoing to make a number of long-distance train trips around Britain, so don't\nbother with a BritRail pass if all you're going to do is make one long-distance\ntrip, or a number of relatively short trips.  For longer distances, a\nBritRail pass typically works out about the same as a normal _Off-Peak_\nflexible ticket bought on the day of travel, it's more expensive than the\ncheaper _Advance_ tickets, but is far cheaper than the _Anytime_ tickets needed to travel in the Monday-Friday\nbusiness peaks, [see \\\\\nmy explanation of British train fares](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#guide-to-train-fares).  So if you want to\nmake early starts on Monday-Fridays and have complete\nall-day flexibility, a BritRail pass is a great idea, but if you're prepared to avoid the Monday-Friday morning &\nafternoon business peaks, and especially if you book cheap\ntickets in advance at\n[tfw.wales](https://www.seat61.com/booking/tfw.htm) (no booking fee!) on a no-refunds,\nno-changes-to-travel-plans basis, ordinary point-to-point\ntickets will be the same or cheaper than a pass.  Before investing in a BritRail pass, check that normal\ntickets wouldn't be cheaper for what you plan to do, using\n[tfw.wales](https://www.seat61.com/booking/tfw.htm)\nor\n[www.nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/) or [www.thetrainline.com](https://www.seat61.com/booking/trainline.htm).\n\n**How to make BritRail seat reservations online**\n\nSeat reservation are optional on all British trains (except overnight sleeper\ntrains).  So you don't have to have one, and indeed can't make one anyway\non many local/rural/urban/suburban routes.  But they are a good idea on\nlong-distance routes, especially at busy times.\n\nDo _not_ pay £££ (or $$$) to make seat reservations through an agency.\nThere are several well-hidden ways to make seat reservations for free to go\nwith a pass (or any other ticket that you have already bought):\n\n- ###### Option 1, at [www.gwr.com](https://www.gwr.com/)\\- can reserve seats on all  reservable trains/operators\n\n\n1.  Go to [www.gwr.com](https://www.gwr.com/).\nMobile _or_ desktop, but _not_ their app.\n\n2.  Click _Sign in /Create_\n_an account_ & register\nfor an account.\n\n3.  When registered and signed\nin, click _My account_.\n\n4.  Click _My journeys_.\n\n5.  Click _Make a reservation_.\n\n6.  A popup appears, click _Next_.\n\n7.  A journey planner appears\nmarked _Select your service_.  Find and select the train you want.\n\n8.  Click _Standard class_\nor _First class_.  Continue and\nmake a reservation.  It says you won't be charged and you'll get a free seat reservation without\nthe need to enter any payment\ndetails.\n\n9.  Your reservation details\nwith car number & seat number will appear on the screen.  Jot them down!\n\nYou don't get (and don't need) a reservation ticket, simply make a note of the\ncar & seat numbers or take a screenshot, although you should also get an email\nconfirmation.  Then board the train and sit in those seats.  The\nconductor won't ask you for any proof of reservation, but you (obviously) need to know where your reserved seat is and you\nmight\nwant to show proof if you find someone sitting in your\nseat.\n\nThis process works for any train, route or operator\non which seat reservations are\npossible, including GWR, LNER, Avanti West Coast, East Midlands Trains, Cross-Country, TransPennine &\nScotrail.\n\n[Please let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if\nthis process changes, but if you _think_ it's ceased to\nwork, first make sure you've followed steps 1 to 5 above!!\n\n- ###### Option 2, at [ticket.tpexpress.co.uk](https://ticket.tpexpress.co.uk/)\\- can reserve seats on all  reservable trains/operators\n\n\n1.  Go to\n[ticket.tpexpress.co.uk](https://ticket.tpexpress.co.uk/).\n\n2.  Use their desktop site or\nthe Trans-Pennine Express app, not their mobile site.  At the time I write\nthis, you seem to get a _Seat_\n_reservations are not available on this service_ message every time when using\ntheir mobile site.\n\n3.  Click _Register_ top right\nand create an account.\n\n4.  When registered and logged\nin, click _Welcome back, \\[your name\\]_ at top right and select _Manage my_\n_tickets / refunds_.\n\n5.  Click on _Make a seat /_\n_bike reservation_ then on the _Make a seat / bike reservation_ button\nwhich appears.\n\n6.  A popup appears, proceed\nto make a seat reservation for free.\n\n7.  To reserve a 2nd class\nseat, select _Anytime single_.  To reserve a 1st class seat, select _Anytime single first class_.\n\n8.  Your reservation details\nwith car number & seat number will appear on the screen.  Jot them down!\n\nYou don't get (and don't need) a reservation ticket, simply make a note of the\ncar & seat numbers or take a screenshot, although you should also get an email\nconfirmation.  Then board the train and sit in those seats.  The\nconductor won't ask you for any proof of reservation, but you (obviously) need to know where your reserved seat is and you\nmight\nwant to show proof if you find someone sitting in your\nseat.\n\nThis process works for any train, route or operator\non which seat reservations are\npossible, including GWR, LNER, Avanti West Coast, East Midlands Trains, Cross-Country, TransPennine &\nScotrail.\n\nSeat reservations can be made for a\nmaximum of 4 journeys per day per account.\n\n[Please let me know](https://www.seat61.com/email.htm) if\nthis process changes, but if you _think_ it's ceased to\nwork, first make sure you've followed steps 1 to 4 above!!\n\n- ###### **Option 3, at** [www.lner.co.uk](https://www.lner.co.uk/travel-information/make-a-reservation/)\\- _only works for LNER_ _trains_\n\n\nIf you want a seat\nreservation on an LNER train (for example, between London & York, Leeds,\nNewcastle, Edinburgh, Inverness, Aberdeen) you can also use\n[www.lner.co.uk/travel-information/make-a-reservation](https://www.lner.co.uk/travel-information/make-a-reservation/)\nto make a free seat reservation up to 5 minutes before the\ntrain leaves its starting station.  You can choose your seat from a seat\nmap on LNER trains if you reserve this way.\n\nYou'll need to register for an\nLNER account during the process, but no payment is necessary, it's free.\nSimply enter _Britrail_ as your booking reference (or make one up,\nwhatever).\n\nHowever, unlike gwr.com or\ntpexpress.co.uk, the LNER website can only make\nreservations for LNER's own trains.\n\n\n**Or make reservations in person**\n\nSimply ask at any staffed station any time up to about 2h before departure,\nor ideally the day before.  There are always places available, even on the\nday.\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n* * *\n\n## Anytime, Off-Peak & Advance fares explained\n\nAll train companies\nuse the same 3 names for the same 3 types of ticket _:  Anytime, Off-Peak_\n_& Advance_.  Fully-flexible, semi-flexible, inflexible.  Here's a\nguide, just make sure you confirm any specific restrictions and conditions when you buy\nyour ticket.\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n| ### **Longer-distance** **fares** |\n| ### **1\\. Anytime**<br>### Fully-flexible.<br>### Any train,<br>### any time. | **Anytime fares** are easy to understand: <br>**Valid any time, any day, any train, any operator**.<br>Anytime fares are expensive full-flex fares aimed at business travellers.<br>For example London-Manchester <br>£193 <br>one-way or £386 return.<br>Anytime fares have <br>unlimited availability and can be bought at the station on the day at the price <br>you see online.  They cannot sell out.  You can turn up, buy a ticket and get on any <br>train you like.  Seat reservation is optional.<br>One-way tickets are valid for 2 days.  Return tickets <br>valid for the outward leg for 5 days, return any time within 1 month.<br>Valid by any permitted route on any operator unless a <br>specific route or operator is shown on the ticket.<br>You can break your journey in either direction.<br>Refundable less an admin fee (usually £5) _until_<br>_midnight the night before_.  This is a new rule starting in April 2026, <br>meaning it's obviously safer to buy on the day just before departure, to avoid <br>losing your money if your plans change at the last minute.  If you need to <br>make a reservation in advance, you can do this before buying a ticket,<br>[use the process shown here](https://www.seat61.com/interrail-and-eurail-reservations.htm#how-to-make-reservations-in-britain).<br>One-way fares normally half <br>the cost of a return.<br>They were called _Open_ <br>fares until 2008. |\n| ### **2\\. Off-Peak**<br>### Semi-flexible,<br>### any train with<br>### time restrictions. | **Off-Peak fares** <br>are valid on any train, <br>any time **except in the Monday-Friday**<br>**business peaks**.<br>They are much more affordable, for travellers who need <br>flexibility but can avoid business hours.<br>For <br>example London-Manchester Super-Off-Peak £79 one-way, £114 return.<br>**The exact time restriction**<br>**varies by route and destination**, so you'll just have to ask what it is - <br>or run a journey planner enquiry and see which trains it is available on.  <br>As a minimum, Off-Peak tickets are <br>valid on any train at weekends, and <br>(it's fairly safe to assume) any train after 10:30 on <br>Monday-Fridays.<br>But they're not usually valid on <br>trains leaving London (or Reading, Watford, Luton or <br>Stevenage) between 15:00-19:00 Mondays-Fridays.<br>However, many Off-Peak tickets have more generous <br>restrictions than this.<br>No advance booking is necessary: Off-Peak fares have a <br>fixed price and unlimited availability so can't sell out, you can buy them at <br>the station on the day at that price, no worries.<br>You can turn up, buy an Off-Peak or Super-Off-Peak ticket and get on any <br>train you like, as long as it's not one of the restricted Monday-Friday <br>peak-time ones.  Seat reservation is optional.<br>Off-Peak returns often cost only a little more than the <br>equivalent Off-Peak one-way, so if you need the flexibility of an Off-Peak <br>ticket on the way back (rather than a train-specific Advance ticket), you may as <br>well pay the extra few pounds and buy an Off-Peak return to use on the outward leg as <br>well.<br>You must <br>travel outward on the date shown <br>on the ticket, but can return any day within 1 month.  <br>You can travel via any permitted <br>route and on any operator's trains, unless a specific route or operator is shown on the <br>ticket.<br>You can break your journey on the <br>return leg, but generally not the outward leg or one a one-way, but this varies <br>so you'll have to check.<br>Refundable less an admin fee (usually £5) _until_<br>_midnight the night before_.  This is a new rule starting in April 2026, <br>meaning it's obviously safer to buy on the day just before departure, to avoid <br>losing your money if your plans change at the last minute.  If you need to <br>make a reservation in advance before buying your ticket,<br>[use the process shown here](https://www.seat61.com/interrail-and-eurail-reservations.htm#how-to-make-reservations-in-britain).<br>**Super-Off-Peak fares:**<br>You may also see a less expensive version called _Super Off Peak_, <br>exactly same principle as Off-Peak, but with tighter time restrictions.<br>Off-Peak fares were called _Saver_ fares before <br>September 2008.<br>_**Update for LNER London <> Newcastle, Edinburgh**:_<br>_LNER have discontinued Off-Peak_<br>_fares for journeys between London and all stations Newcastle to Edinburgh_<br>_inclusive.  It's not a positive move!  But you can still buy them_<br>_[using the workaround shown here](https://www.seat61.com/london-to-edinburgh-by-train.htm#How_much_does_it_cost)._ |\n| ### **3\\. Advance.**<br>### Cheap, inflexible,<br>### specified  train only. | **Advance fares** are also a simple concept to grasp:  Budget-airline-style <br>advance-purchase tickets, **only valid on the specific train you've booked,**<br> <br>**no refunds, limited or no change of travel**<br>**plans**.<br> **Advance tickets are the cheapest option, for example**<br>**London-Manchester from £33 each way.**<br>They have limited availability at each price level, and <br>like budget airline fares the price rises as departure date approaches and the <br>cheaper tickets sell out.  Book early and hunt around the different <br>departures to find the cheapest fare.  In any case, Advance tickets must be booked <br>by 18:00 the day before travel (although in <br>reality they're often still available until 23:59).<br>Advance tickets come with a seat reservation automatically <br>included, you can only travel on that specific train which has been reserved for <br>you.<br>No break of journey is allowed, <br>so you cannot join the train at a station en route, <br>or get off at a station before the one you're booked <br>to.<br>With most Advance fares you can change your departure time <br>or date for a small fee plus difference on fare.  But close to departure, <br>that difference in fare could be significant.<br>Use [www.nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.nationalrail.co.uk/)<br>or a train operator website such as [tfw.wales](https://www.seat61.com/booking/tfw.htm)<br>to check availability.   <br>Advance tickets are all priced as one-way, so you can combine the<br>cheapest fare for your outward journey with<br>the cheapest fare for your return leg |\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n|     |     |\n| --- | --- |\n| ### **Shorter** **distance & London & South** **East fares**<br>The same <br>three types apply to short distances too, though <br>you don't usually find Advance tickets for shorter journeys.  And for short journeys and <br>journeys within the London & Southeast area, Anytime <br>& Off-Peak tickets tend to be valid for just a <br>day, not a month. |\n| ### **1\\. Anytime**<br>### For travel<br>### in the<br>### peak. | Fully-flexible<br>fares, but for shorter journeys valid only <br>for 1 day, not more.  Valid at any time, on any train, by any<br>train operator, by any permitted route (unless a<br>specific route is shown on the ticket).<br>Fixed-price, unlimited availability so can't sell out, can <br>be bought on the day at the same price.<br>You<br>can break your journey in either direction.<br>Refundable less an admin fee (usually £5) _until_<br>_midnight the night before_.  This is a new rule starting in April 2026, <br>meaning it's obviously safer to buy on the day just before departure, to avoid <br>losing your money if your plans change at the last minute. |\n| ### **2\\. Off-Peak**<br>### For travel<br>### in the<br>### off-peak. | **Off-Peak Day returns**:  Short-distance Off-Peak fares are valid 1 day<br>only, on any train at weekends, and<br>after the morning rush hour (about 09:30)<br>on Mondays-Fridays\\*.  After the Monday-Friday<br>morning peak\\*, they are valid on any train, by any<br>train operator, by any permitted route (unless a<br>specific route is shown on the ticket).  Priced for a day out!<br>Fixed-price, unlimited availability so can't sell out, can <br>be bought on the day at the same price.<br>You<br>can break your journey in either direction. <br>Refundable less an admin fee (usually £5) _until_<br>_midnight the night before_.  This is a new rule starting in April 2026, <br>meaning it's obviously safer to buy on the day just before departure, to avoid <br>losing your money if your plans change at the last minute.<br>\\*<br>on routes from London Euston, London Paddington, London Liverpool Street, <br>London Kings <br>Cross, Cardiff, Edinburgh, Glasgow, etc. there is also an evening peak hour<br>restriction out of the city.<br>**Super Off-Peak day returns**:  <br>A few train companies offer a second, cheaper type <br>of off-peak ticket with tighter time restrictions, <br>for example leaving after 1pm.  Restrictions <br>vary by route, so you'll have to ask.<br>**Off-Peak 1 month returns**:  On medium distances, there may also be a <br>one month <br>Off-Peak Return, valid on any train at weekends, <br>after the morning peak hour on Mondays-Fridays), outward travel on the date on the<br>ticket, back within 1 month.  Confusingly, the official name is just _Off-Peak Return_ so if you see both an <br>_Off-Peak Day Return_ and an _Off-Peak_<br>_Return_ with different prices, the latter is probably the one-month version! |\n\n[Back to top](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#top)\n\n### Which  train operators & routes can you use?\n\nPeople get hung\nup on there being 20+ different train operators, but it's actually\npretty simple.  The government requires train operators\nto work together as a network, so:\n\n- If your ticket\nsays 'Route any permitted' (as most of them do) then\nit is valid on any train operator's train, via any\nof the permitted routes between your origin and destination,\nalthough obviously if you have an Off-Peak ticket you must\nstick to any time restrictions which apply to it.\nThere are no time restrictions with an Anytime ticket.\nAn Advance ticket is only valid on the specific train you've\nbooked.\n\n- If your ticket\ngives a specific route, for example 'Route Taunton' then it\nis valid on any train operator's train, via any\nof the permitted routes for that journey that pass\nthrough the specified place, in this case Taunton.\n\n- Only if it says\n(for example) 'Route Avanti West Coast only' are you\nrestricted to using only the trains of that particular train\ncompany, in this example those of Avanti West Coast.  Advance\ntickets are only valid on the specific train you're booked\non, so are by definition only valid on a particular train\ncompany's train - you'll sometimes see the letters 'AP' added to the route, for example 'Route AP\nAvanti West Coast only', AP simply stands for Advance Purchase.  However, it's relatively rare to find\nAnytime & Off-peak fares valid only on trains of a specific\noperator, and where they do exist there are almost always\n'route any permitted' anytime & off-peak fares as well.\n\n\nWhy are\nthere several 'Anytime' & 'Off-Peak' tickets for my journey,\nall with different prices?  Unfortunately, online\nticket systems don't usually show the ticket routing.  So\nyou might see two 'Anytime' fares from (say) London to Hull,\none apparently valid on all the trains, the other only valid\non a handful of trains and cheaper than the first one.  The\nreason is simple, in this case the first one is the 'route any permitted'\nanytime fare, the other is a cheaper 'route Hull Trains only'\nanytime fare only valid on Hull Trains' own services.\nSimilarly, you may find two Anytime or Off-Peak tickets\nbetween London & (say) Birmingham, one valid on all trains,\nthe other only on some of the trains.  In this case it's\nbecause there are two sets of fares to Birmingham, one set\nbeing the 'route any permitted' fares valid on all routes &\noperators, the second being cheaper 'route High Wycombe' fares\nvalid on all operators but only on the slower route via High\nWycombe.  This would be obvious, rather than confusing,\nif the online systems stated the ticket routing for each\nfare they showed, but they don't.  The choice between (on the one\nhand) slower but\ncheaper and (on the other hand) faster, more frequent but more expensive, is up to\nyou.\n\nBreaking your\njourney ('stopovers'):  With an Off-Peak ticket you\ncan take an interesting or scenic route and change trains as\nmuch as you like without leaving the station, as long as you\nstay within the time restrictions which apply to your ticket\nand stick to one of the permitted routes (see below).\nHowever, you can only break your journey (i.e. stop off and\nleave the station) on the return leg of an Off-Peak return,\nnot on the outward leg of an Off-Peak return, and not at all\non an Off-Peak one-way ticket.  With an Anytime one-way\nor return ticket, there are no time restrictions and you can\nbreak your journey anywhere you like along any of the\npermitted routes.\n\nHow are fares\nset?  Here comes the science bit...  Behind the\nscenes, fare setting works like this:  Every\norigin-destination pair (known as a 'flow') is allocated to a\nparticular train operator who has the right and the obligation\nto set the fares.  This operator is known as the 'Lead\nOperator' for that flow.  Once the Lead Operator has set\nthe fares, every other operator serving any part of that flow\n(known as the 'Secondary Operators') are legally obliged to\naccept those fares for travel on their trains.  As I've\nsaid, Advance fares are by definition only valid on one train\noperator's trains, and both Lead Operators and Secondary\nOperators are allowed to set Advance fares for their own\ntrains.  In addition Secondary Operators are allowed to\nset other (more flexible) fare types for their own trains,\nalthough only a few choose to do so.  For example, you'll\nsee 'anytime' & 'off-peak' fares from London to Hull routed\n'Hull Trains only' set by Secondary Operator Hull Trains, in\naddition to the main London to Hull 'route any permitted'\nanytime & off-peak fares set by Lead Operator LNER.  However, Lead Operators are not\npermitted to set fares which are only valid on their own\ntrains, other than Advance fares, temporary fares & first\nclass fares.  Revenue from the fares set by Lead\nOperators is shared between all operators serving that route,\nbased on a computer system called ORCATS which models the\nproportion of passenger miles travelled on each operator.\n\n### What are the  permitted routes?\n\nThe 'permitted routes' for a given\norigin & destination are set out in a document called the\nNational Routeing Guide.  Don't worry about getting hold\nof a copy, even ticket clerks can't understand it half the\ntime, just accept that it exists, and its contents have been\nincorporated into the various journey planning systems such as\nthose used on nationalrail.co.uk, thetrainline.com & raileasy.co.uk.  To give you a flavour of how flexible\nthe permitted routes are:\n\n- The shortest route by regular\ntrain service between the origin and destination is always a\npermitted route;\n\n- You'll generally find that most\nother reasonable routes are permitted, in addition to the\nshortest one.\n\n- If you use a through service (in\nother words, a direct train with no changes) then it is\nalways considered a permitted route as long as you stay on\nthat train, even if the train takes a round-about route;\n\n- From London to\nBirmingham for example, permitted routes are the West\nCoast Main Line from Euston via Milton Keynes, the slower\nroute from Marylebone via High Wycombe & Banbury, and the\nroute from Paddington via Reading, Oxford & Banbury.\n[The\\\\\nrail maps on nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#maps) may help you understand\nthe routes quoted here.\n\n- From London to Edinburgh, Glasgow & most stations in\nScotland for example, the permitted routes include the two obvious main lines\nwith direct trains (East Coast Main Line from Kings Cross via York & Newcastle,\nWest Coast Main Line from Euston via Crewe & Carlisle, plus the Midland Main\nLine from St Pancras via Leicester, Derby or Nottingham, Sheffield, Leeds and\nthe scenic Settle & Carlisle route, and permutations of those routes involving\ntravel from Liverpool Street via Cambridge & Ely to Peterborough then on via\nYork & Newcastle, plus the route from Paddington via Reading, Oxford, Birmingham\n& on via Carlisle.  However, going via Swindon & Gloucester is not\npermitted as it's too far west, nor is going via Ipswich which is too far east.\n[The\\\\\nrail maps on nationalrail.co.uk](https://www.seat61.com/train-travel-in-britain.htm#maps) may help you understand\nthe routes quoted here.\n\n- There are a few quirks in the\nsystem, naturally.  A key one involves London to or\nfrom Devon & Cornwall.  In theory, a 'route any\npermitted' ticket between London & Exeter (or anywhere west\nof Exeter) would be equally valid from Paddington via\nBristol & Taunton or Westbury & Taunton, or on the slower\nscenic route from Waterloo via Salisbury & Honiton.\nHowever, the fares system in fact only contains a set of\nfares routed 'Taunton' and another set routed 'Honiton' as\nthis is what was inherited from British Rail, there aren't\nany 'route any permitted' fares available.  But don't\nworry, under the National Rail Conditions of Carriage you're\nentitled to travel on a different route from the one shown\non your ticket (assuming it's not a train-specific Advance\nticket, of course) on payment of a 'change of route' excess\nfare.  This is the difference, if any, between the fare\npaid for the original route and the cheapest walk-up ticket\nvalid for the train & route now being taken.  Since the\nfaster route via Taunton has the more expensive fares and\nthe tighter time restrictions on its off-peak tickets, this\n'change of route' excess fare normally costs nothing if you\nhave a 'route Taunton' ticket but choose to travel via\nHoniton & Salisbury to/from Waterloo in one or both\ndirections.  If the conductor tries to tell you your\nticket isn't valid, politely suggest he consults his Ticket\nExaminer's Handbook and National Rail Conditions of\nCarriage, and ask for a 'change of route excess fare' at\nzero cost.\n\n\n### London Terminals, London R1256,  London zone U1...?\n\n- When buying a ticket to\nLondon, the cheapest option is to buy a ticket simply to\nthe relevant London station, but for a little more money\nyou can buy a ticket which includes onward Underground\ntravel to an Underground station in a given zone, or\neven unlimited London bus & Underground travel all that\nday.\n\n- A ticket to 'London\nTerminals' (listed in some online booking systems as\n'London mainline stations' or similar) means a ticket\nvalid only as far as the London station where trains\narrive from that particular starting point.  For\nexample, a ticket from Manchester to London Terminals\nwould be valid to London Euston, a ticket from Oxford to\nLondon Terminals would be valid to London Paddington (in\nfact also to London Waterloo as there's an alternative\nroute from Oxford into Waterloo).\n\n- A ticket to 'London R1256',\nwith 'Travelcard' as a ticket type means a ticket\nto London with unlimited bus & Underground travel\nincluded all that day in all 6 London travel zones.\n\n- A ticket to 'London U1'\nis a ticket to London which includes onward travel by\nUnderground to any station you like in Underground zone\n1.  In this case it does not include unlimited\nUnderground travel, just one journey (or a return\njourney if it's a return ticket).\n\n* * *\n\n## Suggested hotels\n\nFor me, a hotel isn't just a roof over my head, I always book something with\ncharacter and history.  Why just visit attractions when you can stay in\none?\n\n### In London\n\n**All-time classics**\n\n**[The Savoy](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-savoy.en-gb.html?aid=339247)**,\n**[Claridge's](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/claridge-s.en-gb.html?aid=339247)**,\n**[the Dorchester](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-dorchester-london.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** and\n**[the Ritz](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-ritz-london.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** are London's are top-end landmark hotels, attractions in their own\nright with a string of famous guests over a century of history.\n\nExpensive,\nbut if you get the chance to stay in one, go for it.  I've been fortunate\nenough to stay at all four, the Savoy on my wedding night (followed by an iconic\ntea at the Ritz).  Since you ask, the art deco Savoy remains my favourite\n(with the best hotel breakfast I have ever had),\nClaridge's is lovely, and the Dorchester was perfect (the receptionist didn't\nblink an eye as Mrs 61 and I checked in with no luggage wearing holed jeans and\nrequesting a toothbrush each, a spur-of-the-moment decision to splurge).  The Ritz's Louis XIV style\nI find a bit ornate!\n\nI should probably add\n**[the Langham](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-langham-london.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** and\n**[the Connaught](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-connaught-mayfair.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** to this list, but I haven't stayed at either place.\n\n**Less pricey but still classics, often with a railway connection**\n\n**[The St Pancras Renaissance Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/renaissance-st-pancras-london.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** is located in the gothic\nsplendour that is St Pancras Station.  It's one of my favourites and I've\nstayed a couple of times, but book with care:  The cheaper rooms (described\nas _contemporary_) are in a modern wing built in the early\n2000s.  To stay in the original historic building at the front of the\nstation that was once the Midland Railway Hotel you must book one of the more\nexpensive _Chambers_ suites with Chambers Club access.  This part of\nthe hotel contains the most beautiful hotel staircase of any hotel, anywhere.\n\n**[The Great Northern Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/great-northern-london.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** is St Pancras' great rival across the\nroad at Kings Cross station.  Less ornate (and a fraction less pricey) I've\nstayed a several times, a great place to stay with free jelly babies in a pantry\non each floor.  And I _like_ jelly babies...\n\n**[The Landmark](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/landmark-london-ld.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** near Marylebone station was once the Great Central Railway\nHotel.  In the 1960s & 70s it was British Rail's HQ, and the\nvast covered atrium in the centre that now contains the lounge and restaurant\nwas then a car park.  I have yet to stay, but have had lunch here several\ntimes.\n\n**[The Charing Cross Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-clermont-charing-cross.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** is perfectly located just off Trafalgar Square,\nideal for a stroll to the National Gallery, Covent Garden or even Whitehall and\nHorse Guard's parade.  Currently branded _The Clermont_, for a\nwhile branded the _Ambla Hotel_, but as a former Station Manager Charing Cross I shall call it by its original\nand proper name,\n[The Charing Cross Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-clermont-charing-cross.en-gb.html?aid=339247).  Another great place to stay - and yes I've stayed\nhere too - and another historic railway hotel.\n\nAnd if we're talking former railway hotels, I rate the\n**[Hilton Paddington](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/hilton-london-paddington.en-gb.html?aid=339247)** too, the former Great Western Royal Hotel in front of\nPaddington station.\n\n### In other cities\n\n**Bath:**  I suggest some hotels\n[here](https://www.seat61.com/london-to-bath-by-train.htm#hotels), including\none in the Royal Crescent itself:  Why just visit an icon when you can stay\nin it?\n\n**York:**  I recommend\n\nthe\n[Principal Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/lemeridianyork.en-gb.html?aid=339247), formerly\nthe York Royal Station\nHotel, a lovely quiet and spacious place set in its own grounds next to York station and only a few\nminutes walk from York's historic centre including the famous _Shambles_.\n[See my \\\\\nphotos here](https://www.seat61.com/london-to-york-by-train.htm#Hotel_in_York).  Also consider the nearby\n[Grand Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/cedar-court-grand.en-gb.html?aid=339247), York's only 5-star hotel, opened in 2010 in what was once\nheadquarters of the North Eastern Railway, built in 1906.\n\n**Manchester:** [The Midland Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/themidland.en-gb.html?aid=339247) was built by the Midland Railway 1898-1904, handy for the\nManchester Central Exhibition Centre (which of course started life as the\nMidland Railway's Manchester Central station).  Charles Rolls met Henry\nRoyce in this hotel, leading to the creation of Rolls-Royce in 1904 and the\nBeatles were infamously refused entry to the French Restaurant for being _inappropriately dressed_.\n\n**Edinburgh:**  I recommend two arch-rivals at each end of Princes Street,\n[The Balmoral](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-balmoral-edinburgh.en-us.html?aid=339247)\nand\n[The Caledonian](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/caledonian-hilton.en-us.html?aid=339247),\nor failing that\n[The Scotsman](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/thescotsmanhotel.en-gb.html?aid=339247).  You can read why I love these three (and which is my favourite) in the\n[hotels section of the London to Edinburgh page](https://www.seat61.com/london-to-edinburgh-by-train.htm#tale_of_two_hotels).\n\n**Glasgow:** Look no further than the iconic\n[Glasgow Central Hotel](https://www.booking.com/hotel/gb/the-grand-central.en-gb.html?aid=339247) (branded _voco Grand Central Hotel_ since 2021),\nbuilt in 1883 by the Caledonian Railway as part of Glasgow Central station.\nThe world's first long-distance television pictures were transmitted to the\nhotel on 24 May 1927 by John Logie Baird.  The hotel staircase once\nsurrounded Europe's tallest chandelier - until someone in Budapest installed a\ntaller one!  I am still looking for an excuse to stay here!\n\n|     |\n| --- |\n| ### [![Find hotels at Booking.com](https://www.seat61.com/images2/bookingdotcom1.jpg)](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247) My favourite hotel  search: _[www.booking.com](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247)_<br>[Booking.com](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247) <br>is my favourite hotel booking site and<br>I generally use it to book all my hotels in one place.  I've come to trust<br>[booking.com](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247)'s review scores, you won't be disappointed with <br>any hotel that scores 8.0 or more.  Crucially,<br>[booking.com](https://www.booking.com/index.html?aid=339247) <br>usually lets you book with **free**<br>**cancellation,** which means you can confirm accommodation risk-free before <br>train booking opens and/or you can hold accommodation while you finalise <br>your itinerary and alter your plans as they evolve - a feature I use all the <br>time when planning a trip.  I never book hotels non-refundably! |\n\n### Backpacker hostels: [www.hostelworld.com](https://prf.hn/click/camref:1011lIIm/creativeref:1011l8373) **[![](https://www.seat61.com/images/Hostelbookers_logo_200.gif)](https://prf.hn/click/camref:1011lIIm/creativeref:1011l8373)**\n\nIf you're on a tight budget,\ndon't forget the hostels.  For a dorm bed or an\nultra-cheap private room in backpacker hostels in most\nEuropean cities use\n[www.hostelworld.com](https://prf.hn/click/camref:1011lIIm/creativeref:1011l8373).\n\n**### [![](https://www.seat61.com/images2/sykes-cottages.jpg)](https://www.awin1.com/awclick.php?gid=192107&mid=3317&awinaffid=92204&linkid=405289&clickref=)Rent a cottage**\n\n\n**Renting a house or cottage for a week or two is one of my own favourite family**\n**breaks.  However, unfortunately I'm no longer able to promote www.sykescottages.co.uk.**\n\n* * *\n\n[Back to See how much you could save today!\\\\\n\\\\\nFind out more](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/tickets-discounts/season-tickets)\n\n[![Careers icon](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/linkable_blocks_icon/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/image-2-20250522-093507-revised.png?itok=wLMUjXB-)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Careers at EMR**\\\\\n\\\\\nLooking for an exciting career opportunity? A range of parking options are available, whether you need a daily ticket, monthly or annual. Find out more by visiting our Car Parking page.\\\\\n\\\\\nFind out more](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/trains-stations/at-the-station/car-parking)\n\n![EMR app screenshot](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/phone-min.png?itok=vJZG5ek2)\n\n## A simpler way to travel\n\nGet the best price train tickets, check journey information in real-time and save with no booking fees on the EMR app.\n\n[See all features](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/tickets-discounts/download-our-app)\n\n![Download me to scan](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/revised-qr-final.png?itok=QGvtkEDV)\n\n[![Download from the App Store](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/download_app_store.png?itok=UDNwituv)](https://apps.apple.com/gb/app/emr-east-midlands-railway/id1472202499)[![Download from Google Play](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/download_google_play.png?itok=diGAPjqo)](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=uk.co.eastmidlandsrailway.app&amp;amp;hl=en_GB&amp;amp;pli=1)\n\n![Miles holding a phone](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2026-04/miles-phone-2026%20%282%29_0.jpg?itok=q5nyOeTc)\n\n[![Trax logo](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-06/MKTG00961%20TRAX1146x672.jpg?itok=3Bm6GAXC)\\\\\n\\\\\n**TRAX - we keep business moving**\\\\\nFind the right train ticket for you with our flexible ticket options](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/form/corporate-season-tickets)\n\n[![Fare Freeze](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2026-03/Homepage%20tile%201146x672%20%281%29%20%281%29.png?itok=A0Gg4iix)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Train fares frozen until March 2027**\\\\\nAcross Standard class tickets including Seasons, Off-Peak and Anytime](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/rail-fares-freeze)\n\n[![2 for 1 for on city cruises ](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2026-09/COMM01540%20Resize%20for%20website%20V2_RGB_Citycruises.jpg?itok=be6MDh-q)\\\\\n\\\\\n**2 for 1 on top London attractions**\\\\\nSave big on London attractions when you travel by train. Find out more](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/discover/offers)\n\n## Travel updates\n\n[Loading...](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/#self)\n\n![Megaphone icon](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/max_width/public/assets/media/image/image/2025-07/Frame.png?itok=r8WeaDC6)\n\n## Be one of the first to know about our latest offers\n\n[Sign up to our mailing list](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/form/join-our-mailing-list)\n\n[![Refresh your commute save with a flexi season](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2026-09/COMM01540%20Resize%20for%20website%20V2_RGB_Flexi.jpg?itok=zvxOTg7Q)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Flexible train tickets to suit you**\\\\\nFind the right train ticket for you with our flexible ticket options](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/tickets-discounts/flexi-season)\n\n[![Plan your journey](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2024-01/MKTG00460%20Homepage%20blocks%20update-800x450_PLAN%20YOUR%20JOURNEY.png?itok=X_npaIID)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Plan your journey**\\\\\nPlan your journey from door to door. Find options for travel to and from the station, on foot, by bus, tram or car](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/#)\n\n[![Bid win relax with First Class upgrades with Seatfrog](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/sites/default/files/styles/card_image/public/assets/media/image/image/2026-09/COMM01540%20Resize%20for%20website%20V2_RGB_Seatfrog.jpg?itok=u2YWPI_5)\\\\\n\\\\\n**First Class upgrades with Seatfrog**\\\\\nSee how you can get a First Class upgrade](https://www.eastmidlandsrailway.co.uk/trains-stations/on-board-the-train/seatfrog)",

Don't worry, if you change your mind you can opt out at any time.\n\n### Subscription confirmed\n\nThank you for subscribing to receive relevant updates from South Western Railway.\n\nOkay\n\n## I want to...\n\n[SWR Careers](https://careers.southwesternrailway.com/) [Check live train times](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/train-times) [Check engineering works](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/plan-my-journey/planned-improvements) [Book travel assistance](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/travelling-with-us/assisted-travel) [Be inspired](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/destinations-and-offers/destinations) [Read the latest news](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/other/news-and-media)\n\nJJJFMAASONDM\n\n[![South Western Railway](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/Assets/img/SWR-logo.svg)](https://www.southwesternrailway.com/ \"\")\n\nreCAPTCHA",
